be the classical Jacobi polynomials and let

be the classical incomplete inner

as described in

. For

(
188
) and

using

189
) by taking

\pi_{i}\to\hat{\Pi}_{s-1,i},\rho_{ij}\to\hat{\mathrm{P}}_{s-1,ij},t\to t_{\ell_{s}}
. Define

\tilde{\tau}_{s}(x),\hat{\tau}_{s}(x),\tilde{T}_{s},\hat{T}_{s}\;s=1,\ldots q_{1}-1
using
(
186
) with
\alpha\to a,\beta\to b
. By Lemmas

8.17
and
8.18
, and by induction, the
\hat{\tau}_{s}

are polynomials, the
\hat{P}_{s,i}
are rational eigenfunctions of

\hat{T}_{s}
, and the
\hat{\mathrm{P}}_{s,ij}
the corresponding incomplete inner
products. The operators
\tilde{T}_{s},\hat{T}_{s}
form a

-step chain of
type 1,2 CDTs

\hat{T}_{s-1}\mathrlap{\xleftharpoondown{\phantom{\lambda_{s}}}}\xrightharpoonup{\lambda_{s}}\tilde{T}_{s}\mathrlap{\xleftharpoondown{\phantom{\lambda_{s}}}}\xrightharpoonup{\lambda_{s}}\hat{T}_{s},\quad\lambda_{s}=\lambda_{1}(\ell_{s};a,b),\;s=1,\ldots,q_{1}.

Next, let
\alpha_{s}=a,\;\beta_{s}=b+2(s-q_{1}),\;s=q_{1},\ldots q_{1}+q_{3}-1

and inductively define

\hat{\Pi}_{s;i}(x;\boldsymbol{t}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}),\hat{\mathrm{P}}_{s;ij}(x;\boldsymbol{t}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}),\;s=q_{1},\ldots,q_{1}+q_{3}-1,\;i,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
using (
176
)
(

) by taking

\pi_{i}\to\hat{\Pi}_{s-1,i},\;\rho_{ij}\to\hat{\mathrm{P}}_{s-1,ij},\ell\to\ell_{s}
. Then, use (

) to define

\hat{\tau}_{s}(x),\;s=q_{1},\ldots,q_{1}+q_{3}-1
with

\tau\to\hat{\tau}_{s-1},\;\rho_{\ell\ell}\to\hat{\mathrm{P}}_{s-1,\ell_{s}\ell_{s}},\;\beta\to\beta_{s}
, and define the
operators

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{s}

\displaystyle={T_{\mathrm{rg}}}(\tilde{\tau}_{s},\alpha_{s-1}+1,\beta_{s-1}+1)+(s-q_{1})(a+b+s-q_{1}+1),

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{s}

\displaystyle={T_{\mathrm{rg}}}(\hat{\tau}_{s},\alpha_{s},\beta_{s})+(s-q_{1})(a+b+s-q_{1}+1)

By Lemmas

and

, and by induction, the

\hat{\tau}_{s}
are polynomials, the
\hat{P}_{s,i}
are rational

\hat{T}_{s}

\hat{\mathrm{P}}_{s,ij}
the corresponding

\hat{T}_{s-1}\mathrlap{\xleftharpoondown{\phantom{\lambda_{s}}}}\xrightharpoonup{\lambda_{s}}\tilde{T}_{s}\mathrlap{\xleftharpoondown{\phantom{\lambda_{s}}}}\xrightharpoonup{\lambda_{s}}\hat{T}_{s},\quad\lambda_{s}=\lambda_{1}(\ell_{s};a,b),\;s=q_{1}+1,\ldots,q_{1}+q_{3}.

Next, let
\alpha_{s}=a+2(s-q_{1}-q_{3}),\;\beta=b+2q_{3},\;s=q_{1}+q_{3}+1,\ldots,q_{1}+q_{3}+q_{4}

and inductively define

\hat{\Pi}_{s;i}(x;\boldsymbol{t}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}),\hat{\mathrm{P}}_{s;ij}(x;\boldsymbol{t}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}),\;s=q_{1}+q_{3},\ldots,q_{1}+q_{3}+q_{4}-1,\;i,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
using (

)
(

) by taking

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{s}

\displaystyle={T_{\mathrm{rg}}}(\tilde{\tau}_{s},\alpha_{s-1}+1,\beta_{s-1}+1)+(s-q_{1}-q_{3})(a+b+s-q_{1}-q_{3}+1),

\displaystyle T_{s}

\displaystyle={T_{\mathrm{rg}}}(\tau_{s},\alpha_{s},\beta_{s})+(s-q_{1}-q_{3})(a+b+s-q_{1}-q_{3}+1).

Lemma 8.24
.

Let
\hat{\tau},\,{\hat{\alpha}},{\hat{\beta}},\,\hat{\pi}_{i},\;i\in\hat{I}_{1}
be as in
Lemma
8.23
. The corresponding norms are given by

(208)

\displaystyle\hat{\nu}_{i}

\displaystyle=\hat{\kappa}(\hat{n}_{i})\nu(\hat{n}_{i};a,b)\quad\text{where }

\displaystyle\hat{\kappa}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\ell\in L_{1}}\left(1-\delta_{z,\ell^{*}}\frac{{\tilde{\nu}}_{\ell}}{t_{\ell}+{\tilde{\nu}}_{\ell}}\right)\prod_{\ell\in L_{34}}\!\!\left(\frac{z-\ell^{*}}{z-\ell}\right)^{2},\quad{\tilde{\nu}}_{\ell}=\nu(\ell;a,b).

Proof.

This follows by Lemma
8.16
and by
(
206
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.19
.

The subcase where
p=0
is covered by Lemma
8.23
. The
formula for
\tau
in (
89
) follows by Lemma

8.13
. The same Lemma justifies the degree formula
(
91
). The formula for the quasi-polynomial
eigenfunctions given in (
90
) is justified by Proposition

4.13
. The form of the normalization constant
\chi

follows by Lemma
4.22
. The norm formula (
92
)
(
93
) follows from
(
208
) of Lemma
8.24
and by (
169
)
of Lemma
8.7
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.20
.

By Theorem
2.34
, by Propositions
7.10

7.12
and by Proposition
7.18
, the given
T

is Darboux-connected to a classical
T(a,b)
, where

(a,b)\in\{(0,0),(1,0),(0,1)\}
. The first possibility holds if

\alpha=\beta
; the second possibility holds if
\alpha>\beta
; and the last
possibility holds if
\alpha<\beta
. Hence, by Proposition

7.18
,
\Lambda_{T}
is a finite modification of such a

\Lambda(a,b)
. The construction and uniqueness of the parameters

K,L_{1},L_{3},L_{4},a,b
is spelled out in Remark
3.21
. The form
of
\tau
then follows by the argument detailed about in the proof
of Proposition
3.19
.
∎

8.7.
Proof of Proposition
2.11
and Theorems
2.30
and
2.33

Proof of Proposition
2.11
.

By assumption, the exceptional operator in question belongs to one
of the ABCD classes. The desired conclusion now follows by the
Propositions stated in section
3
. For example, for a
class G operator, Proposition
3.3
asserts that the
T
in
question has the form described in Proposition
3.2
. That
same Proposition asserts that (
23
) holds for such
operators. The proof is given earlier in this section. The same
reasoning and justification applies to the operators in the other
degeneracy classes.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.30
.

The theorem is a direct consequence of the Propositions in Section

3
. For the G class, the theorem follows from
Propositions
3.2
and
3.3
. For the A class, the
theorem follows by Propositions
3.6
and
3.7
.
For the B class, the theorem follows by Propositions
3.10

and
3.11
. For the C and CB classes, the theorem follows
by Propositions
3.14

3.15
and
3.16
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.33
.

By the Propositions detailed in the preceding proof, the norms of
the quasi-polynomials of GABC operators are fully determined in
terms of their degree. Therefore, an isospectral deformation cannot
exists for such operators. For the D class, the desired conclusion
is a consequence of Propositions
3.19
and
3.20
.
The one-to-one correspondence between extended spectral diagrams and
isospectral deformations is also a consequence of Propositions

3.19
and
3.20
. By (
208
) of Lemma

8.24
, for
i\in I_{1-}
we have

\nu_{i}=\frac{t_{\ell}}{t_{\ell}+{\tilde{\nu}}_{\ell}}\times\text{const.},\quad\text{where }\ell=i^{\star}+p+q_{3}+q_{4}.

Thus, the mapping

t_{\ell}\mapsto\nu_{i}
is one-to-one. The conclusion now follows
because the support of the isospectral deformation is a finite set.
∎
